{"forms":[{"form":"vernaculars","tags":["plural"]}],"lang_code":"en","pos":"noun","senses":[{"examples":[{"text":"The principal vernacular of the United States is English.","type":"example"},{"text":"The idea that the Bible should be translated into vernaculars was explosive in medieval society.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["The language of a people or a national language."],"synonyms":[{"word":"vulgate"},{"word":"vulgar"}]},{"examples":[{"text":"Street vernacular can be quite different from what is heard elsewhere.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Everyday speech or dialect, including colloquialisms, as opposed to standard, literary, liturgical, or scientific idiom."],"synonyms":[{"word":"basilect"},{"word":"demotic"}]},{"examples":[{"text":"For those of a certain age, hiphop vernacular might just as well be a foreign language.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Language unique to a particular group of people."],"synonyms":[{"word":"jargon"},{"word":"argot"},{"word":"dialect"},{"word":"slang"}]},{"glosses":["A language lacking standardization or a written form."]},{"examples":[{"text":"Vatican II, a church council in the 1960s, allowed the celebration of the mass in the vernacular.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Indigenous spoken language, as distinct from a literary or liturgical language such as Ecclesiastical Latin."],"synonyms":[{"word":"vulgate"},{"word":"vulgar"}]},{"glosses":["A style of architecture involving local building materials and styles; not imported."],"topics":["architecture"]}],"sounds":[{"ipa":"/vəˈnækjələ/","tags":["UK"]},{"ipa":"/-jʊlə/","tags":["UK"]},{"ipa":"/vɚˈnækjəlɚ/","tags":["US"]}],"upstream_line":24114,"upstream_offset":166251574,"upstream_record_sha256":"ffb303ea7b2397e3f87872c2b62eeff87aa0a04baad819ac711513845314a214","word":"vernacular"}
